### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Match List-I with List-II.<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-I
Electronic configuration of neutral atom (where $n=2$)</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">List-II
$1^{st}$ Ionization Energy (kJ mol$^{-1}$)</th></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">A. $ns^2$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">I. $2080$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">B. $ns^2np^1$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">II. $899$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">C. $ns^2np^3$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">III. $800$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">D. $ns^2np^6$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">IV. $1402$</td></tr></table>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I
- **(B)** A-IV, B-III, C-II, D-I
- **(C)** A-III, B-II, C-IV, D-I
- **(D)** A-III, B-II, C-I, D-IV

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $n=2$, the electronic configurations correspond to elements of the second period.

A. $ns^2 \Rightarrow 2s^2$ is Beryllium (Be).
B. $ns^2np^1 \Rightarrow 2s^2 2p^1$ is Boron (B).
C. $ns^2np^3 \Rightarrow 2s^2 2p^3$ is Nitrogen (N).
D. $ns^2np^6 \Rightarrow 2s^2 2p^6$ is Neon (Ne).

The first ionization energy generally increases across a period from left to right. However, Beryllium has a higher ionization energy than Boron due to the stable fully-filled $2s$ subshell. Nitrogen has a high ionization energy due to its stable half-filled $2p$ subshell. Neon, being a noble gas, has the highest ionization energy.

The order of first ionization energy is B $<$ Be $<$ N $<$ Ne.

Arranging the given values in increasing order: $800 < 899 < 1402 < 2080$ kJ mol$^{-1}$.

Thus, the matching is:
A. Be $\rightarrow 899$ (II)
B. B $\rightarrow 800$ (III)
C. N $\rightarrow 1402$ (IV)
D. Ne $\rightarrow 2080$ (I)

This corresponds to A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I.

Answer: A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In a period, the first ionisation enthalpy of the element at extreme left and the negative electron gain enthalpy of the extreme right element, except noble gases, are respectively.

**Options:**

- **(A)** lowest and lowest
- **(B)** highest and lowest
- **(C)** lowest and highest
- **(D)** highest and highest

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) lowest and highest**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a period, as we move from left to right, the atomic size decreases and the effective nuclear charge increases.

Due to the large atomic size and low effective nuclear charge, the element at the extreme left (alkali metal) has the lowest first ionisation enthalpy in the period.

As we move to the extreme right (excluding noble gases), the elements (halogens) have the smallest atomic size and highest effective nuclear charge in the period. They require only one electron to complete their octet. Thus, they have the highest negative electron gain enthalpy.

Therefore, the first ionisation enthalpy of the extreme left element is the lowest, and the negative electron gain enthalpy of the extreme right element is the highest.

Answer: lowest and highest

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:

Statement I: Aluminium is more electropositive than thallium as the standard electrode potential value of $E^{\circ}_{\text{Al}^{3+}/\text{Al}}$ is negative and $E^{\circ}_{\text{Tl}^{3+}/\text{Tl}}$ is positive.

Statement II: The sum of first three ionization enthalpies of boron is very high when compared to that of aluminium. Due to this reason boron forms covalent compounds only and aluminium forms $\text{Al}^{3+}$ ion.

In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: The standard reduction potential $E^{\circ}_{\text{Al}^{3+}/\text{Al}}$ is $-1.66$ V, which is highly negative, indicating that aluminium has a strong tendency to lose electrons and is highly electropositive. The standard reduction potential $E^{\circ}_{\text{Tl}^{3+}/\text{Tl}}$ is $+1.26$ V, which is positive, indicating that thallium is less electropositive than aluminium. Thus, Statement I is true.

Statement II: Boron has a very small atomic size, resulting in a very high sum of its first three ionization enthalpies. This high energy requirement prevents boron from losing three electrons to form $\text{B}^{3+}$ ions, so it forms only covalent compounds. In contrast, aluminium has a larger size and a significantly lower sum of its first three ionization enthalpies, allowing it to easily form $\text{Al}^{3+}$ ions in its compounds. Thus, Statement II is true.

Both Statement I and Statement II are true.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are true

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
Statement I: The number of pairs among $[Al_2O_3, Cr_2O_3]$, $[Cl_2O_7, Mn_2O_7]$, $[Na_2O, V_2O_3]$ and $[CO, N_2O]$ that contain oxides of same nature (acidic, basic, neutral or amphoteric) is $4$.
Statement II: Among $Na_2O$, $Al_2O_3$, $CO$ and $Cl_2O_7$, the most basic and acidic oxides are $Na_2O$ and $Cl_2O_7$, respectively.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I:
$Al_2O_3$ and $Cr_2O_3$ are both amphoteric oxides.
$Cl_2O_7$ and $Mn_2O_7$ are both acidic oxides.
$Na_2O$ and $V_2O_3$ are both basic oxides.
$CO$ and $N_2O$ are both neutral oxides.
All $4$ pairs contain oxides of the same nature. Hence, Statement I is true.

Statement II:
$Na_2O$ is a strongly basic oxide.
$Al_2O_3$ is an amphoteric oxide.
$CO$ is a neutral oxide.
$Cl_2O_7$ is a strongly acidic oxide.
Thus, the most basic oxide is $Na_2O$ and the most acidic oxide is $Cl_2O_7$. Hence, Statement II is true.

Both Statement I and Statement II are true.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are true

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: The correct order of electronegativity of fluorine, oxygen and nitrogen is $F > O > N$.
Statement II: The oxidation state of oxygen in OF$_2$ is $+2$ and in Na$_2$O is $-2$.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Electronegativity increases across a period from left to right. Nitrogen, oxygen, and fluorine belong to group 15, 16, and 17 respectively in the second period. Thus, the correct order of electronegativity is $F > O > N$. Statement I is true.

In $OF_2$, fluorine is more electronegative than oxygen and always exhibits an oxidation state of $-1$. Let the oxidation state of oxygen be $x$. Then $x + 2(-1) = 0 \Rightarrow x = +2$.

In $Na_2O$, sodium is an alkali metal and exhibits an oxidation state of $+1$. Let the oxidation state of oxygen be $y$. Then $2(+1) + y = 0 \Rightarrow y = -2$.

Statement II is also true.

Both Statement I and Statement II are true.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are true

</details>

---

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The electronegativity of a group 13 element 'E' is same as that of Ge (on Pauling scale and upto one decimal point). The CORRECT statements about $E^{3+}$ are
A. It can act as a reducing agent.
B. It can act as an oxidizing agent.
C. $E^{3+}$ is more stable than $E^+$.
D. The standard electrode potential value for $E^{3+}/E$ is positive.
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and C Only
- **(B)** B and C Only
- **(C)** B and D Only
- **(D)** A and D Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) B and D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

The electronegativity of Ge on the Pauling scale is $1.8$. In Group 13, the electronegativity values are B ($2.0$), Al ($1.5$), Ga ($1.6$), In ($1.7$), and Tl ($1.8$). Thus, the element 'E' is Thallium (Tl).

Due to the inert pair effect, the $+1$ oxidation state is much more stable than the $+3$ oxidation state for the heavier elements of Group 13, especially Thallium. Therefore, $Tl^+$ is more stable than $Tl^{3+}$. This makes statement C incorrect.

Since $Tl^{3+}$ is relatively unstable, it readily accepts two electrons to form the more stable $Tl^+$ ion ($Tl^{3+} + 2e^- \rightarrow Tl^+$). Because it undergoes reduction easily, $Tl^{3+}$ acts as a strong oxidizing agent. This makes statement A incorrect and statement B correct.

The standard electrode potential for the reduction of $Tl^{3+}$ to Tl ($E^{\circ}_{Tl^{3+}/Tl}$) is $+1.26\text{ V}$, which is a positive value. This makes statement D correct.

Therefore, only statements B and D are correct.

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The $1^{st}$ ionization enthalpy for Mg is $+737$ kJ/mol. The most probable estimated value of the $2^{nd}$ ionization enthalpy of Mg is ______.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-906$ kJ/mol
- **(B)** $-856$ kJ/mol
- **(C)** $+1450$ kJ/mol
- **(D)** $+590$ kJ/mol

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $+1450$ kJ/mol**

**Detailed Solution:**

Ionization enthalpy is the energy required to remove an electron from an isolated gaseous atom or ion. It is always an endothermic process, so its value is always positive.

The second ionization enthalpy is the energy required to remove an electron from a unipositive ion. Since the effective nuclear charge increases after the removal of the first electron, the remaining electrons are held more tightly by the nucleus. Thus, the second ionization enthalpy is always greater than the first ionization enthalpy ($IE_{2} > IE_{1}$).

Given the first ionization enthalpy of Mg is $+737$ kJ/mol, the second ionization enthalpy must be positive and greater than $+737$ kJ/mol.

Among the given options, only $+1450$ kJ/mol satisfies these conditions.

Answer: $+1450$ kJ/mol

</details>

---

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: Among $Zn, Mn, Sc$ and $Cu$, the energy required to remove the third valence electron is highest for $Zn$ and lowest for $Sc$.
Statement II: The correct order of the following complexes in terms of CFSE is $[Co(H_2 O)_6]^{2+} < [Co(H_2 O)_6]^{3+} < [Co(en)_3]^{3+}$.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I:
The third ionization energy ($IE_3$) corresponds to the removal of the third valence electron. Let us look at the electronic configurations of the divalent cations for the given metals:
$Sc^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^{1}$
$Mn^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^{5}$
$Cu^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^{9}$
$Zn^{2+}$: $[Ar] 3d^{10}$

For $Sc$, removing the third electron involves taking it from the $3d^{1}$ state, which results in a highly stable noble gas core ($[Ar]$). Hence, $Sc$ has the lowest $IE_3$.
For $Zn$, the third electron must be removed from a completely filled, highly stable $3d^{10}$ subshell. Combined with its high effective nuclear charge ($Z_{eff}$) across the 3d series, $Zn$ requires the highest energy for the removal of the third electron.
Thus, Statement I is true.

Statement II:
The Crystal Field Stabilization Energy (CFSE) depends on the magnitude of the crystal field splitting energy ($\Delta_o$), which in turn depends on the oxidation state of the central metal ion and the strength of the ligand.
1. Oxidation state: A higher oxidation state on the central metal ion leads to a larger $\Delta_o$. Therefore, the splitting for $Co^{3+}$ complexes is significantly greater than for $Co^{2+}$ complexes. This makes the CFSE of $[Co(H_2O)_6]^{3+}$ greater than that of $[Co(H_2O)_6]^{2+}$.
2. Nature of the ligand: Ethylenediamine ($en$) is a strong field bidentate ligand, whereas $H_2O$ is a weak field ligand. According to the spectrochemical series, $en$ causes a much larger splitting than $H_2O$. Thus, the $\Delta_o$ and CFSE for $[Co(en)_3]^{3+}$ are greater than those for $[Co(H_2O)_6]^{3+}$.

The overall order of CFSE is:
$[Co(H_2O)_6]^{2+} < [Co(H_2O)_6]^{3+} < [Co(en)_3]^{3+}$
Thus, Statement II is true.

Since both statements are correct, the correct option is (1).

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are true

</details>

---

### Q9. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: The second ionization enthalpy of B, Al and Ga is in the order of $B > Al > Ga$.
Statement II: The correct order in terms of first ionization enthalpy is $Si < Ge < Pb < Sn$.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Both Statement I and Statement II are false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: The second ionization enthalpy of Ga is higher than that of Al. This is due to the poor shielding effect of the ten $3d$ electrons in Ga, which increases the effective nuclear charge and holds the valence electrons more tightly. The correct order of second ionization enthalpy is $B > Ga > Al$. Thus, Statement I is false.

Statement II: The first ionization enthalpy generally decreases down the group. However, Pb has a slightly higher ionization enthalpy than Sn due to the poor shielding of $4f$ and $5d$ electrons (lanthanoid contraction). The correct order of first ionization enthalpy for Group 14 elements is $Sn < Pb < Ge < Si < C$. Thus, Statement II is false.

Both Statement I and Statement II are false.

Answer: Both Statement I and Statement II are false

</details>

---

### Q10. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
<b>Statement (I) :</b> The first ionisation enthalpy of the elements Na, Mg, Cl and Ar follows the order Na $>$ Mg $>$ Cl $>$ Ar.
<b>Statement (II) :</b> Among Ca, Al, Fe and B, the third ionisation enthalpy is very high for Ca.
In the light of the above statements, choose the <b>correct answer</b> from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

The first ionisation enthalpy generally increases across a period from left to right due to an increase in effective nuclear charge. For the third period elements $\text{Na}$, $\text{Mg}$, $\text{Cl}$, and $\text{Ar}$, the correct order of first ionisation enthalpy is $\text{Ar} > \text{Cl} > \text{Mg} > \text{Na}$. Thus, Statement I is false.

The electronic configuration of $\text{Ca}$ is $[\text{Ar}] 4s^2$. After the removal of two electrons, $\text{Ca}^{2+}$ attains the highly stable noble gas configuration of Argon $[\text{Ar}]$. Removing a third electron requires breaking this stable noble gas core, which requires a very large amount of energy. Therefore, the third ionisation enthalpy of $\text{Ca}$ is exceptionally high compared to $\text{Al}$, $\text{Fe}$, and $\text{B}$. Thus, Statement II is true.

Answer: Statement I is false but Statement II is true

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Consider the elements $\mathrm{N}, \mathrm{P}, \mathrm{O}, \mathrm{S}, \mathrm{Cl}$ and F. The number of valence electrons present in the elements with most and least metallic character from the above list is respectively.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 7 and 5
- **(B)** 5 and 7
- **(C)** 5 and 6
- **(D)** 6 and 7

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 5 and 7**

**Detailed Solution:**

Valence electrons: N(5), P(5), O(6), S(6), Cl(7), F(7).
Metallic character order in the list: P > S > N > Cl > O > F.
Most metallic character: P with 5 valence electrons.
Least metallic character (most nonmetallic): F with 7 valence electrons.
F is the most electronegative and has the smallest atomic size, making it most nonmetallic.

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In period 4 of the periodic table, the elements with highest and lowest atomic radii are respectively.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Rb & Br
- **(B)** K & Se
- **(C)** $\mathrm{K} ~\&~ \mathrm{Br}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Na} ~\&~ \mathrm{Cl}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{K} ~\&~ \mathrm{Br}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In period 4, atomic radii decrease from left to right as nuclear charge increases while electron shell remains constant. K (potassium) is at the extreme left of period 4 metals and has the highest atomic radius. Br (bromine) is near the right end as a nonmetal and has the lowest atomic radius in this period. Therefore, the elements with highest and lowest atomic radii in period 4 are K and Br respectively.

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Choose the INCORRECT statement

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{CO}_{2}$ is the most acidic oxide among the dioxides of group of 14 elements.
- **(B)** Carbon exhibits negative oxidation states along with +4 and +2.
- **(C)** Carbon cannot exceed its covalency more than four.
- **(D)** Among the isotopes of carbon, ${ }^{13} \mathrm{C}$ is a radioactive isotope.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Among the isotopes of carbon, ${ }^{13} \mathrm{C}$ is a radioactive isotope.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Analyzing each statement:
<b>1</b> CO₂ is most acidic among group 14 dioxides - correct, it's a non-metal acidic oxide.
<b>2</b> Carbon shows negative oxidation states (-4 in methane, -3 in ethane, etc.) along with +2 and +4 - correct.
<b>3</b> Carbon covalency cannot exceed 4 - correct, as it's period 2 element unable to expand octet.
<b>4</b> ¹³C is radioactive - incorrect. ¹³C is a stable, non-radioactive isotope. ¹⁴C is the radioactive isotope used in radiocarbon dating with half-life of 5730 years. Therefore, statement (4) is incorrect.

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of $\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{N}, \mathrm{O}$ and F in terms of second ionisation potential is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{C}<\mathrm{N}<\mathrm{F}<\mathrm{O}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{C}<\mathrm{O}<\mathrm{N}<\mathrm{F}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{C}<\mathrm{F}<\mathrm{N}<\mathrm{O}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{F}<\mathrm{N}<\mathrm{C}<\mathrm{O}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{C}<\mathrm{N}<\mathrm{F}<\mathrm{O}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Second ionization potential (I₂) is the energy to remove the second electron.
After removing the first electron: C⁺ has 2p¹, N⁺ has 2p², O⁺ has 2p³ (half-filled, stable), F⁺ has 2p⁴. 
 C has lowest I₂ as 2p electrons are least tightly bound with lowest nuclear charge.
N has slightly higher I₂ than C due to increased nuclear charge.
F has highest I₂ due to highest effective nuclear charge.
O⁺ with half-filled 2p is relatively stable, making I₂ of O intermediate. 
 Order: $C < N < F < O$

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: $\mathrm{K}>\mathrm{Mg}>\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{B}$ is the correct order in terms of metallic character. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: Atomic radius is always greater than the ionic radius for any element. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is true but Statement II is false**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I: Metallic character decreases from left to right across a period and increases down a group. K (Group 1, Period 4) is the most metallic element among these. Mg (Group 2, Period 3) is less metallic than K. Al (Group 13, Period 3) is less metallic than Mg. B (Group 13, Period 2) is a metalloid with the least metallic character. The order K > Mg > Al > B correctly represents decreasing metallic character. TRUE. 

Statement II: Atomic radius compared to ionic radius depends on whether the ion is a cation or anion. For metals forming cations: atomic radius > cationic radius (e.g., Na > Na⁺). However, for nonmetals forming anions: atomic radius < anionic radius (e.g., Cl < Cl⁻). The statement claims atomic radius is ALWAYS greater for ANY element, which is incorrect because anions have larger radii than their parent atoms. FALSE. 

Correct answer: Statement I is true but Statement II is false.

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: The second ionisation enthalpy of Na is larger than the corresponding ionisation enthalpy of Mg. 
  <b>Statement II</b>: The ionic radius of $\mathrm{O}^{2-}$ is larger than that of $\mathrm{F}^{-}$. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I examines second ionization enthalpy:
Na (1s$^2$ 2s$^2$ 2p$^6$ 3s$^1$) loses its first electron to achieve a stable noble gas configuration. The second ionization would break into the 2p core, requiring enormous energy.
Mg (1s$^2$ 2s$^2$ 2p$^6$ 3s$^2$) loses its second electron from the 3s orbital, which is less stable.
Thus, IE$_2$ of Na is much larger than IE$_2$ of Mg.
Statement I is TRUE.
Statement II compares O$^{2-}$ and F$^-$:
both are isoelectronic with 10 electrons.
O$^{2-}$ has 8 protons while F$^-$ has 9 protons.
The lower nuclear charge in O$^{2-}$ results in weaker electrostatic attraction, making the ionic radius of O$^{2-}$ larger than F$^-$.
Statement II is TRUE.

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Elements X and Y belong to Group 15. The difference between the electronegativity values of ' X ' and phosphorus is higher than that of the difference between phosphorus and ' Y '. ' X ' \& ' Y ' are respectively

**Options:**

- **(A)** N & As
- **(B)** $\mathrm{As} ~\&~ \mathrm{Sb}$
- **(C)** Bi & N
- **(D)** As & Bi

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) N & As**

**Detailed Solution:**

Group 15 elements have electronegativity values (Pauling scale): N = 3.04, P = 2.19, As = 2.18, Sb = 2.05, Bi = 2.02.
<b>The condition requires: |EN(X) - EN(P)| > |EN(P) - EN(Y)|.</b>
<b>(1)</b> N and As: |3.04 - 2.19| = 0.85 and |2.19 - 2.18| = 0.01. Since 0.85 > 0.01, the condition is satisfied.
<b>(2)</b> As and Sb: |2.18 - 2.19| = 0.01 and |2.19 - 2.05| = 0.14. Since 0.01 < 0.14, it fails.
<b>(3)</b> Bi and N: |2.02 - 2.19| = 0.17 and |2.19 - 3.04| = 0.85. Since 0.17 < 0.85, it fails.
<b>(4)</b> As and Bi: |2.18 - 2.19| = 0.01 and |2.19 - 2.02| = 0.17. Since 0.01 < 0.17, it fails.

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct trend in the first ionization enthalpies of the elements in the $3^{\text {rd }}$ period of periodic table is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Si}<\mathrm{S}<\mathrm{Al}<\mathrm{P}<\mathrm{Cl}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Al}<\mathrm{S}<\mathrm{P}<\mathrm{Si}<\mathrm{Cl}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Al}<\mathrm{Si}<\mathrm{S}<\mathrm{P}<\mathrm{Cl}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{S}<\mathrm{Si}<\mathrm{Al}<\mathrm{P}<\mathrm{Cl}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{Al}<\mathrm{Si}<\mathrm{S}<\mathrm{P}<\mathrm{Cl}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

First ionization enthalpy generally increases across a period due to increasing nuclear charge and decreasing atomic radius. However, exceptions occur based on electron configuration stability.
For 3rd period elements, the approximate ionization enthalpy values are: Al (578), Si (787), P (1012), S (1000), Cl (1251) kJ/mol.
(1) Al has lower IE than Si because removing 3p¹ is easier than removing one electron from 3p².
(2) S has slightly lower IE than P despite being to the right because P has a half-filled p-orbital (3p³) which is more stable.
(3) Cl has the highest IE as it approaches a filled p-orbital.
The correct order is: Al < Si < S < P < Cl

</details>

---

### Q19. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements : 
 <b>Statement I</b>: $\quad \mathrm{C}<\mathrm{O}<\mathrm{N}<\mathrm{F}$ is the correct order in terms of first ionization enthalpy values.  
 <b>Statement II</b>: $\quad \mathrm{S}>\mathrm{Se}>\mathrm{Te}>\mathrm{Po}>\mathrm{O}$ is the correct order in terms of the magnitude of electron gain enthalpy values. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(B)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Statement I:
Ionization enthalpy generally increases across a period from left to right.
However, Nitrogen ($2s^2 2p^3$) has a stable half-filled electronic configuration, making it harder to remove an electron compared to Oxygen ($2s^2 2p^4$).
Thus, the order is $C < O < N < F$.
Statement I is true.

Statement II:
For Group 16 elements, Oxygen has an exceptionally low magnitude of electron gain enthalpy due to its small size and high inter-electronic repulsions in the $2p$ subshell.
The magnitude of electron gain enthalpy decreases down the group from Sulphur onwards.
The correct order of magnitude is $S > Se > Te > Po > O$.
Statement II is true.

Both statements are correct.

</details>

Given below are two statements: 
 <b>Statement I</b>: The halogen that makes longest bond with hydrogen in HX, has the smallest covalent radius in its group. 
 <b>Statement II</b>: A group 15 element's hydride $\mathrm{EH}_{3}$ has the lowest boiling point among corresponding hydrides of other group 15 elements. The maximum covalency of that element E is 4. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below

- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

$\mathrm{HF}<\mathrm{HCl}<\mathrm{HBr}<\mathrm{HI}$ (bond length order)

$\mathrm{PH}_3<\mathrm{AsH}_3<\mathrm{NH}_3<\mathrm{SbH}_3<\mathrm{BiH}_3$ (Boiling point order)

### Q21. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
 <b>Statement I</b> : The correct order in terms of atomic/ionic radii is $\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{Mg}>\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}>\mathrm{Al}^{3+}$. 
 <b>Statement II</b> : The correct order in terms of the magnitude of electron gain enthalpy is $\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{Br}>\mathrm{S}>\mathrm{O}$. 
 In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(D)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

Analyzing Statement I: The order given is Al > Mg > Mg²⁺ > Al³⁺. However, the correct atomic radii order should be Mg > Al because Mg is in group 2 (period 3) while Al is in group 13 (period 3), and moving right across a period decreases radius. The correct order would be Mg > Al > Mg²⁺ > Al³⁺. Therefore, Statement I is FALSE.

Analyzing Statement II: Electron gain enthalpy magnitudes follow: Cl (highest, ~349 kJ/mol) > Br (~325) > S (~200) > O (~141). This is because Cl has the highest tendency to gain an electron due to its position and electronic configuration. Statement II is TRUE.

Since Statement I is false and Statement II is true, the answer is option (3).

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following represents the correct trend for the mentioned property ? 
 A. $\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{P}>\mathrm{S}>\mathrm{B}$ $\ \ \ $ - $\ \ \ $ First Ionization Energy 
 B.  $\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{S}>\mathrm{P}$ $\ \ \ $ -  $\ \ \ $ Electron Affinity 
 C. $\mathrm{K}>\mathrm{Al}>\mathrm{Mg}>\mathrm{B}$ $\ \ \ $ - $\ \ \ $ Metallic character 
 D. $\mathrm{K}_{2} \mathrm{O}>\mathrm{Na}_{2} \mathrm{O}>\mathrm{MgO}>\mathrm{Al}_{2} \mathrm{O}_{3} \quad$ $\ \ \ $ - $\ \ \ $ Basic character 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** B and C only
- **(B)** A, B and D only
- **(C)** A and B only
- **(D)** A, B, C and D

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A, B and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

A. F > P > S > B (IE₁): F has highest IE₁, P > S due to half-filled $3p^3$ stability, B has lowest. Correct 
B. Cl > F > S > P (EA): Cl has highest EA (F smaller, more repulsion), S > P (half-filled p-orbital). Correct 
C. K > Al > Mg > B (Metallic character): Incorrect. Mg (Group 2) is more metallic than Al (Group 13) in same period. Correct order: K > Mg > Al > B 
D. $K_2O > Na_2O > MgO > Al_2O_3$ (Basic character): Follows electropositive nature. $Al_2O_3$ is amphoteric. Correct 
Correct statements: A, B and D only.

</details>

Density : $\mathrm{Tl} \lt \mathrm{In} \lt \mathrm{Ga} \lt \mathrm{Al} \lt \mathrm{B}$

(B) $1 \mathrm{~s}^2 2 \mathrm{~s}^2 2 \mathrm{p}^4$
(C) $1 \mathrm{~s}^2 2 \mathrm{~s}^2 2 \mathrm{p}^5$
(D) $1 \mathrm{~s}^2 2 \mathrm{~s}^2 2 \mathrm{p}^2$
Which of the following is the correct order of increasing electronegativity (Pauling's scale)?

**Options:**

- **(A)** A &lt; D &lt; B &lt; C
- **(B)** A &lt; C &lt; B &lt; D
- **(C)** A &lt; B &lt; C &lt; D
- **(D)** D &lt; A &lt; B &lt; C

### Q30. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

### Q35. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The successive 5 ionisation energies of an element are $800,2427,3658,25024$ and $32824 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$, respectively. By using the above values predict the group in which the above element is present :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Group 13
- **(B)** Group 14
- **(C)** Group 2
- **(D)** Group 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Group 13**

**Detailed Solution:**

The successive 5 ionisation energies of an element are $800,2427,3658,25024$ and $32824 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$, respectively.
$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\mathrm{IE}_2}{\mathrm{IE}_1}=\frac{2427}{800}=3.03 \\
& \frac{\mathrm{IE}}{\mathrm{IE}_2}=\frac{3658}{2427}=1.51 \\
& \frac{\mathrm{IE}}{\mathrm{IE}_3}=\frac{25024}{3658}=6.84
\end{aligned}$
$\frac{\mathrm{IE}_5}{\mathrm{IE}_4}=\frac{32824}{25024}=1.31$
Since ( $\mathrm{IE}_4 / \mathrm{IE}_3$ ) value is maximum, the element belongs to group 13.

</details>

### Q39. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Match List I with List II
\(\begin{array}{|l|l|c|l|}
\hline & \text{ List - I } & & \text{List - II} \\
\hline \text { A. } & \text { Melting Point }[\mathrm{K}] & \text { I. } & \mathrm{T} 1>\mathrm{In}>\mathrm{Ga}>\mathrm{A} 1>\mathrm{B} \\
\hline \text { B. } & \text { Ionic Radius }\left[\mathrm{M}^{+3} / \mathrm{pm}\right] & \text { II. } & \mathrm{B}>\mathrm{T} 1>\mathrm{A} 1 \approx \mathrm{Ga}>\mathrm{In} \\
\hline \text { C. } & \Delta_{\mathrm{i}} \mathrm{H}_1\left[\mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right] & \text { III. } & \mathrm{T} 1>\mathrm{In}>\mathrm{A} 1>\mathrm{Ga}>\mathrm{B} \\
\hline \text { D. } & \text { Atomic Radius }[\mathrm{pm}] & \text { IV. } & \mathrm{B}>\mathrm{A} 1>\mathrm{T} 1>\mathrm{In}>\mathrm{Ga} \\
\hline
\end{array}\)
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I
- **(B)** A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III
- **(C)** A-I, B-II, C-III, D-IV
- **(D)** A-III, B-IV, C-I, D-II

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A-IV, B-I, C-II, D-III**

**Detailed Solution:**

Melting point : $\mathrm{B}>\mathrm{A} \ell>\mathrm{T} \ell>\mathrm{In}>\mathrm{Ga}$
Ionic radius $\left(\mathrm{M}^{+3} / \mathrm{pm}\right): \mathrm{T} \ell>\mathrm{In}>\mathrm{Ga}>\mathrm{A} \ell>\mathrm{B}$
$\left(\Delta_{\mathrm{IE}} \mathrm{H}\right)_1\left[\frac{\mathrm{kJ}}{\mathrm{mol}}\right]: \mathrm{B}>\mathrm{T} \ell>\mathrm{A} \ell \approx \mathrm{Ga}>\mathrm{In}$
Atomic radius (in $\mathrm{pm}$ ) : $\mathrm{T} \ell>\mathrm{In}>\mathrm{A} \ell>\mathrm{Ga}>\mathrm{B}$

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Match List I with List II
\(\begin{array}{|l|l|c|l|}
\hline & \text{List - I} & & \text{List - II} \\
\hline & \text{(Elements)} & & \text{(Properties in their respective groups)} \\
\hline \text { A. } & \mathrm{Cl}, \mathrm{S} & \text { I. } & \text { Elements with highest electronegativity } \\
\hline \text { B. } & \mathrm{Ge}, \mathrm{As} & \text { II. } & \text { Elements with largest atomic size } \\
\hline \text { C. } & \text { Fr, Ra } & \text { III. } & \begin{array}{l}
\text { Elements which show properties of both } \\
\text { metals and non-metal }
\end{array} \\
\hline \text { D. } & \text { F, O } & \text { IV. } & \begin{array}{l}
\text { Elements with highest negative electron } \\
\text { gain enthalpy }
\end{array} \\
\hline
\end{array}\)
Choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A-II, B-I, C-IV, D-III
- **(B)** A-III, B-II, C-I, D-IV
- **(C)** A-IV, B-III, C-II, D-I
- **(D)** A-II, B-III, C-IV, D-I

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) A-IV, B-III, C-II, D-I**

**Detailed Solution:**

Elements with highest electronegativity $\rightarrow \mathrm{F}, \mathrm{O}$
Elements with largest atomic size $\rightarrow \mathrm{Fr}, \mathrm{Ra}$
Elements which shows properties of both metal and non-metals i.e. metalloids $\rightarrow \mathrm{Ge}$, As
Elements with highest negative electron gain enthalpy $\rightarrow \mathrm{Cl}, \mathrm{S}$

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 2)

### Q50. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given below are two statements :
Statement (I) : Both metal and non-metal exist in $\mathrm{p}$ and d-block elements.
Statement (II) : Non-metals have higher ionisation enthalpy and higher electronegativity than the metals.
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate answer from the option given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(B)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true
- **(C)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>I. In p-Block both metals and non metals are present but in d-Block only metals are present</p><p>p-block elements: The elements which are present in group <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>13</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn></math>. The elements in p-blocks are of all kind metals and non-metals.
Transition elements: Those elements which are in groups from three to twelve. They are called a transition because they are in between the s- block elements and p-block elements. For example, scandium, iron, zinc, etc. Transition elements are also known as d-block elements.</p><p>II. EN and IE of non-metals are greater than those of metals.</p><p>Ionization Energy:<strong> </strong>The amount of energy required to remove an electron from a gaseous atom is known as ionization energy.</p><ul> <li>The size of non-metals is small as compared to metals due to which electrons are more tightly held in non-metals.</li> <li>Non-Metals are electronegative elements and tend to share or accept electrons in a chemical reaction.</li> <li>Due to the electrons accepting nature of non-metals, they gains more electrons to complete their octet.</li> <li>So, non-metals have high ionization enthalpy due to their small size and high electronegativity as compared to metal.</li></ul><p>I - False, II-True</p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Lowest Oxidation number of an atom in a compound <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo></math> The number of an electron in its valence shell is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>→</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> has complete octet in its dianionic form, thus in its atomic state it has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math> electrons in its valence shell. As it has negative charge, it has acquired two electrons to complete its octet. If the lowest oxidation state of an atom is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, this typically implies that the atom can gain two electrons to reach a stable configuration. Therefore, the number of valence electrons in that atom would be six, because gaining two electrons would fill its outer shell.</p>

</details>

---

### Q52. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In case of isoelectronic species the size of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>–</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi>Ne</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is affected by:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Principal quantum number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** None of the factors because their size is the same
- **(C)** Electron-electron interaction in the outer orbitals
- **(D)** Nuclear charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Nuclear charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>Ne</mi><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>all have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></math>configuration. They have different size due to the difference in nuclear charge.</p><p>For isoelectronic species, the more nuclear charges, the less will be the size of the species. The valence electrons will experience a greater attractive force due to the increase in nuclear charge.</p><p>Hence, the size of isoelectronic species - <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><mi>Ne</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is affected by nuclear charge <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></mfenced></math>.</p><p>Thus, the ionic size of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is the lowest, and the ionic size of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> ion is the highest.</p><p>Hence, the answer is D.</p>

</details>

---

### Q53. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Consider the following elements.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0ba10fd1-2991-4eb9-9972-0d900218840d.png" style="width: 237px; height: 74px;"/></p><p>Which of the following is/are true about <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> ?</p><p>A. Order of atomic radii: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math></p><p>B. Order of metallic character : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math></p><p>C. Size of the element : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math></p><p>D. Order of ionic radii : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup></math></p><p>Choose the correct answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A only
- **(B)** A, B and D only
- **(C)** A and B only
- **(D)** B, C and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A, B and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In a group the atomic size increases due to the addition of shells as we move from one period to another. Across a period the atomic size decreases as the number of shells remain the same while the nuclear charge increases. This leads to the pulling of electrons from the outermost shell towards the nucleus thereby decreasing the size.</p><p> Metallic character increases as we move down the group because the atomic size increases which lead to easy loss of electrons. On the other hand, it decreases across a period as we move from left to right. This happens because there is an increase in nuclear charge which makes it difficult for an atom to lose electrons.</p><p>In general along the period from left to right, size decreases and metallic character decreases.</p><p>In general down the group, size increases and metallic character increases.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> (size) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> (size)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> (size) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&gt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> (size)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> (metallic character)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup></math> (metallic character)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup></math> (size)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>'</mo></msup><mo>+</mo></msup></math> (size)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∴</mo></math> C statement is incorrect.</p><p>Hence ,option B is the answer.</p>

</details>

---

### Q54. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>The correct sequence of electron gain enthalpy of the elements listed below is</p><p>A. Ar  B. Br  C. F  D. S</p><p>Choose the most appropriate from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** C &gt; B &gt; D &gt; A
- **(B)** A &gt; D &gt; B &gt; C
- **(C)** A &gt; D &gt; C &gt; B
- **(D)** D &gt; C &gt; B &gt; A

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A &gt; D &gt; B &gt; C**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Electron gain enthalpy is defined as the amount of energy released when an electron is added to an isolated gaseous atom.</p><p>Electron gain enthalpy: On moving from left to right, atomic size decreases and effective nuclear charge increases. Thus, electron gain enthalpy becomes more negative. On moving down the group, atomic size increases. Thus, electron gain enthalpy becomes less negative.</p><table border="1" cellpadding="1" cellspacing="1" style="width: 500px;"> <tbody> <tr> <td>Element</td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mo>∆</mo><mrow><mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow></msub><mi>H</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>J</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>)</mo></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math></td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>–</mo><mn>333</mn></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math></td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>–</mo><mn>200</mn></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>r</mi></math></td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>–</mo><mn>325</mn></math></td> </tr> <tr> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>r</mi></math></td> <td><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>96</mn></math></td> </tr> </tbody></table>

</details>

---

### Q55. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given below are two statements:
Statement - I: Along the period, the chemical reactivity of the element gradually increases from group <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> to group <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn></math> .
Statement - II: The nature of oxides formed by group <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> element is basic while that of group <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>17</mn></math> elements is acidic.
In the the light above statements, choose the most appropriate from the questions given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both statement I and Statement II are true.
- **(B)** Statement I is true but Statement II is False.
- **(C)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true.
- **(D)** Both Statement I and Statement II is false.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Statement I is false but Statement II is true.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The atomic and ionic radii, as we know, generally decrease in a period from left to right. As a consequence, the ionisation enthalpies generally increase (with some exceptions) and electron gain enthalpies become more negative across a period. In other words, the ionisation enthalpy of the extreme left element for a period is the least and the electron gain enthalpy of the element on the extreme right is the highest negative (Note: noble gases having completely filled shells have rather positive electron gain enthalpy values). This results in high chemical reactivity at the two extremes and the lowest in the centre. </p><p>
Group - 1 elements form basic nature oxides.  Metallic oxides are basic in nature because they react with dilute acids to form salt and water, while group - <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>17</mn></math> elements form acidic oxides, An oxide that exhibits the property of acids is called acidic oxide. Since they are acidic, they react with a base to form a salt. Non-metals react with oxygen to form acidic compounds of oxides which are held together by covalent bonds. Therefore, statement - II is true.</p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q56. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are the two statements: one is labeled as Assertion (A) and the other is labeled as Reason (R).</p><p>Assertion (A): There is a considerable increase in covalent radius from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>. However from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>s</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>i</mi></math> only a small increase in covalent radius is observed.</p><p>Reason (R): covalent and ionic radii in a particular oxidation state increases down the group.</p><p>In the light of the above statement, choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A) is false but (R) is true
- **(B)** Both (A) and (R) are true but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)
- **(C)** (A) is true but (R) is false
- **(D)** Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Both (A) and (R) are true but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>There is a considerable increase in covalent radius from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>. However, from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>As</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Bi</mi></math> only a small increase in covalent radius is observed. This is due to the presence of completely filled d and/or f orbitals in heavier members.</p><p>As going down in a group, the number of shells increases, hence the covalent and ionic radii increases.</p><p>Both (A) and (R) are true but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)</p><p>But the correct explanation is the presence of completely filled d and f-orbitals of heavier members.</p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements:
Statement I: Fluorine has most negative electron gain enthalpy in its group.
Statement II: Oxygen has least negative electron gain enthalpy in its group.
In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate from the options given below.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(B)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Statement I is false but Statement II is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Statement-1 is false because chlorine has most negative electron gain enthalpy in its group.</p><p>The size of fluorine is comparatively smaller due to which there exists force of repulsion  between the valence electrons and they also repel the incoming electron  but the size of chlorine is comparatively larger and it easily attains one electron to become stable
Thus, the electron gain enthalpy of chlorine is more negative than that of fluorine.</p><p>Oxygen has least negative electron gain enthalpy in its group, due to its small size. As a result of which the electron -electron repulsion in the relatively small <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math>p subshell are comparatively large and hence the incoming electrons are not accepted with the same ease in case of other elements of the group.</p><p>Hence option D is the answer.</p>

</details>

---

### Q58. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The element having the highest first ionization enthalpy is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Si</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The first ionisation energy is the energy needed to remove the outermost, or highest energy, electron from a neutral atom in the gas phase. </p><p>The ionisation energy decreases from top to bottom in groups, and increases from left to right across a period. Nitrogen and carbon belong to second period elements, hence they have more ionisation energy than the other two elements.</p><p>It is highest for nitrogen because nitrogen is a more stable half-filled electronic configuration.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>IE</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> order.</p><p>Hence option C is the answer.</p>

</details>

---

### Q59. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Given below are two statements : one is labelled as Assertion A and the other is labelled as Reason R:</p><p>Assertion A: The first ionisation enthalpy decreases across a period.</p><p>Reason R: The increasing nuclear charge outweighs the shielding across the period.</p><p>In the light of the above statements, choose the most appropriate from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A
- **(B)** A is true but R is false
- **(C)** A is false but R is true
- **(D)** Both A and R are true but R is NOT the correct explanation of A

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) A is false but R is true**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Ionization enthalpy is defined as the minimum amount of energy that is required to remove the most loosely bounded electron that is the electron present in the outermost shell, from an isolated gaseous atom.</p><p>First, ionisation energy increases along the period because effective nuclear charge increases. Along the period <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> increases which outweighs the shielding effect.</p><p>Hence, option C is the answer.</p>

</details>

---

### Q60. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

. Identify the correct order of standard enthalpy of formation of sodium halides.

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaI</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaF</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaI</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaF</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaF</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaI</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaF</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaI</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaI</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaF</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The lattice energy of an ionic compound is the energy required to separate one mole of the compound into its constituent ions in the gas phase.</p><p> Lattice energy<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mo>+</mo></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mo>-</mo></msub></mrow></mfenced></mfrac></math></p><p>The lattice energy is inversely proportional to the sum of the ionic radii <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>+</mo></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>-</mo></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> of the ions involved. When the size of the ions increases, the distance between them also increases, leading to a decrease in the attractive forces between the ions and a decrease in the lattice energy. Therefore, if the size of the ions increases, the lattice energy generally decreases.</p><p>So Order : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaF</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaCl</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaBr</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>NaI</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q61. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Which of the following statements are not correct?</p><p>A. The electron gain enthalpy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> is more negative than that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>B. Ionization enthalpy decreases in a group of periodic table.</p><p>C. The electronegativity of an atom depends upon the atoms bonded to it.</p><p>D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math> are examples of amphoteric oxides.</p><p>Choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, B, C and D
- **(B)** A, B and D only
- **(C)** B and D only
- **(D)** A, C and D only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) A, C and D only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>A is incorrect. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi></math> belong to the same group of the periodic table. The electron gain enthalpy usually becomes less negative on moving down a group. However, in this case, the value of the electron gain enthalpy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi></math> is more negative than that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>. This is because the atomic size of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> is smaller than that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi></math>. </p><p>B is correct (ionisation energy decreases down the group).</p><p>C is incorrect (Electronegativity is a fixed property of an element and is typically measured on scales such as the Pauling scale or the Mulliken scale.). </p><p>D is incorrect. The oxides which behave as both acidic and basic oxides are called amphoteric oxides. . Here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is amphoteric oxide and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NO</mi></math> is neutral oxide.</p><p>Hence, A, C and D are incorrect.</p>

</details>

---

### Q62. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

For elements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Li</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>and</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>, the correct order of first ionisation enthalpy is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Li</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Li</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Li</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Li</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Li</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The first ionisation energy varies predictably across the periodic table. As we move from left to right across a period, the ionisation energy of elements increases. This is due to the decrease in the size of atoms across a period. The valence electrons get closer to the nucleus of an atom as we move from left to right due to increased nuclear charge. The force of attraction between the nucleus and the electrons increases and hence more energy is required to remove an electron from the valence shell. Ionisation energy of boron being unexpectedly less than that for beryllium due to the 2s orbital being totally filled in beryllium, whereas boron has one electron in a 2p orbital as well, and the 2s orbital is shielded much more than the 2p orbital. Ionisation energy is more for nitrogen than oxygen because nitrogen is more stable due to its half-filled electronic configuration.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Li</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q63. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

For compound having the formula <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>GaAlCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>, the correct option from the following is

**Options:**

- **(A)** Ga is coordinated with CI in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>GaAlCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** Ga is more electronegative than AI and is present as a cationic part of the salt <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>GaAlCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** CI forms bond with both AI and Ga in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>GaAlCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** Oxidation state of Ga in the salt <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>GaAlCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>is</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Ga is more electronegative than AI and is present as a cationic part of the salt <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>GaAlCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Gallous tetrachloro aluminate (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>GaAlCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math>) exists as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Ga</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><msubsup><mi>AlCl</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo></msubsup></math>.</p><p>Due to the presence of d-orbital in the case of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ga</mi></math>, the shielding effect of the d-orbital electrons is very poor so the size of the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ga</mi></math> atom is smaller than expected and so it can attract the shared pairs of electrons more effectively. Hence, the electronegativity of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ga</mi></math> is more than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

---

### Q64. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order of metallic character is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ca</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#62;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Be</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The metallic character of an element is defined as the easiness of its atom in losing electrons. According to the modern periodic table, the metallic character of an element decreases while moving from left to right across a period. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> is an alkali metal. For alkaline earth metals, metallic character increases down the group. Hence, the correct order of metallic character is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Be</mi></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>
