### Q92. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>The ionic radii of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> respectively are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>33</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>, while the covalent radius of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>74</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> </p><p>The correct statement for the ionic radius of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> from the following is :</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** It is smaller than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>
- **(B)** It is bigger than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>
- **(C)** It is bigger than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>, but smaller than of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** It is smaller than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>, but bigger than of
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) It is bigger than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> all are isoelectronic species in which <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> have least number of protons due to which it&#39;s size increases as least nuclear attraction
is experienced by the outer shell electrons. Size order <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>

</details>

### Q93. JEE Main 2021 (25 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The ionic radii of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> are in the order :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> are isoelectronic ionic species. For monatomic ionic isoelectronic species as positive charge increases ionic size decreases.</p><p>The order of size of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#38;</mo><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mo>+</mo></msup></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Increasing order of ionic radii:  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The set in which compounds have different nature is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Be</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

1) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math> acidic and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> acidic
2) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math> acidic and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math> amphoteric
3) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi></math> basic and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></math> basic
4) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Be</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msub></math> amphoteric and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>OH</mi><msub><mo>)</mo><mn>3</mn></msub></math> amphoteric

</details>

### Q95. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The first ionization energy of magnesium is smaller, as compared to that of elements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math>, but higher than that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>. The elements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>, respectively, are :

**Options:**

- **(A)** chlorine, lithium and sodium
- **(B)** argon, lithium and sodium
- **(C)** argon, chlorine and sodium
- **(D)** neon, sodium and chlorine

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) argon, chlorine and sodium**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>1</mn><mi>st</mi></msup></math> IE order of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>3</mn><mi>rd</mi></msup></math> period is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Na</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ar</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ar</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Cl</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> is sodium <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>Na</mi><mo>)</mo></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q96. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The ionic radius of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> ions is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>02</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></mover><mo>.</mo></math> The ionic radii (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></mover></math>) of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> respectively, are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>99</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>54</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>85</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>99</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>68</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>72</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>54</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we know nuclear charge increases, attraction of nucleus on electron increases because of that size decreases.</p><p>Here all ions have same number of electrons so as the number of protons increases nuclear charge increases and size decreases.</p><p>The ionic radii order is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#62;</mo><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q97. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The absolute value of the electron gain enthalpy of halogens satisfies:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Order of electron gain enthalpy</p><p>(Absolute value)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q98. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The characteristics of elements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> with atomic numbers, respectively, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>33</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>53</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>83</mn></math> are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> are metalloids and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> is a metal
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> is a metalloid, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> is a non-metal and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> is a metal.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> are metals.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> are non-metals and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> is a metalloid

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> is a metalloid, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> is a non-metal and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> is a metal.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>As</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>33</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mo>&#8594;</mo></math> Metalloid</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mmultiscripts><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>53</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mo>&#8594;</mo></math> Non-metal</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>=</mo><mmultiscripts><mi>Bi</mi><mprescripts></mprescripts><mn>83</mn><none></none></mmultiscripts><mo>&#8594;</mo></math> Metal</p>

</details>

### Q99. JEE Main 2021 (16 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p>Identify the elements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> using the ionization energy values given below :</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable><mtr><mtd/><mtd><mi>Ionization</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>energy</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>(</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>)</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd/><mtd><msup><mn>1</mn><mi>st</mi></msup></mtd><mtd><msup><mn>2</mn><mi>nd</mi></msup></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>495</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>4563</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>731</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1450</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Na</mi><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Mg</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Na</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Mg</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Na</mi><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Mg</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Na</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ne</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><msub><mi>IE</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is very low but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>IE</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is very high due to stable noble gas configuration of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mi>Ne</mi></mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msub><mi>IE</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>IE</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo></math> Low</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>IE</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is very high.</p>

</details>

---

### Q100. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which pair of oxides is acidic in nature?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>CaO</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>BaO</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CaO</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>SiO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>SiO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>SiO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>An oxide that combines with water to give an acid is termed as an acidic oxide. The oxide that gives a base in water is known as a basic oxide. An amphoteric solution is a substance that can chemically react as either acid or base.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>SiO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> both are oxides of non-metal and hence are acidic in nature.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="" open="{"><mtable columnalign="left"><mtr><mtd><mi>CaO</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>BaO</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Basic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Nature</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>SiO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>Acidic</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Nature</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>Neutral</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>oxide</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q101. JEE Main 2021 (26 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order of electron gain enthalpy is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Te</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Te</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Te</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Te</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Te</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Electron gain enthalpy is the amount of energy released when an element accepts an electron. The process is endothermic.</p><p>correct order of electron gain enthalpy is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Te</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Te</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p>(Oxygen shows least electron gain enthalpy due to small size of atom)</p>

</details>

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>(a) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>Be</mi></math></p><p>(b) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math></p><p>(c) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p><p>(d) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>2</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mn>1</mn></msup><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math></p><p>The ionization enthalpy order is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Be</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Be</mi></math> has more <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>IE</mi></math> compared to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> due to extra stability <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi></math> has more <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>IE</mi></math> compared to oxygen due to extra stability Hence,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>1402</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi ### Q103. JEE Main 2021 (24 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the elements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mg</mi><mo>,</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Si</mi><mo>,</mo></math> the correct increasing order of their first ionisation enthalpy is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>In general from left to right in a period, ionisation enthalpy increases due to effective nuclear charge increases. but due to extra stability of half filled and full filled electronic configuration, required ionisation enthalpy is more from neighbouring elements.
i.e. first ionisation enthalpy order is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Si</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q104. JEE Main 2020 (05 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order of the ionic radii of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>*</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>AI</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>AI</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>AI</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Al</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Mg</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#60;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math></p><p>All are isoelectronic species so more is the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math> of less will be the ionic size.</p>

</details>

### Q107. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The five successive ionization enthalpies of an element are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>800</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2427</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3658</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>35024</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>32824</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> . The number of valence electrons in the element is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 5
- **(B)** 4
- **(C)** 3
- **(D)** 2

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 3**

**Detailed Solution:**

As difference in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>3</mn><mi>rd</mi></msup></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>4</mn><mi>th</mi></msup></math> ionisation energies is high so atom contains <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math> valence electrons.

</details>

### Q108. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Three elements <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> are in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>3</mn><mtext>rd&#160;</mtext></msup></math>period of the periodic table. The oxides of<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>, respectively, are basic, amphoteric and acidic. The correct order of the atomic numbers of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

On moving left to right in a period. Acidic character of oxides is increase. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mn>3</mn><mtext>rd&#160;</mtext></msup></math> period element oxides. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><munder><munder><mrow><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow><mo>&#9183;</mo></munder><mtext>Basic&#160;</mtext></munder><mi>MgO</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><munder><munder><mrow><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow><mo>&#9183;</mo></munder><mtext>Amphoteric&#160;</mtext></munder><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><munder><munder><mrow><msub><mi>SiO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>5</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>7</mn></mrow><mo>&#9183;</mo></munder><mtext>Acidic&#160;</mtext></munder></math>
(i) Acidic character<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8593;</mo></math>
(i) Atomic No<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8593;</mo></math>so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> have minimum Atomic No
&amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math> have maxima Atomic No So correct order is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Y</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></math>

</details>

### Q109. JEE Main 2020 (02 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In general, the property (magnitudes only) that shows an opposite trend in comparison to other properties across a period is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** lonization enthalpy
- **(B)** Electronegativity
- **(C)** Electron gain enthalpy
- **(D)** Atomic radius

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Atomic radius**

**Detailed Solution:**

On moving Left to Right along a period. Atomic Radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> decreases. Electronegativity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math>Increases. Electron gainenthalpy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Increases. Ionisation Enthalpy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Increases.

</details>

### Q110. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The first and second ionisation enthalpies of a metal are <math><mn>496</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4560</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>, respectively. How many moles of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo></math> respectively, will be needed to react completely with <math><mn>1</mn></math> mole of the metal hydroxide?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2</mn></math> and <math><mn>0.5</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mn>0.5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mn>0.5</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Ionization Enthalpy values indicate that the metal belongs to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi>st</mi></msup></math> group, since the second ionization enthalpy is very high.
(Q - only one valence electron)
Metal hydroxide will be of the type, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MOH</mi></math>.

<math> <mrow> <munder> <mrow> <mtext>MOH</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>mol</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </munder> <mo>+</mo><munder> <mrow> <mtext>HCl</mtext></mrow> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <mn>1</mn><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>mol</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </munder> <mo>&#8594;</mo><mtext>MCl</mtext><mo>+</mo><msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>2</mtext> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>MOH</mtext><mo>+</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mrow> <mtext>SO</mtext></mrow> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <mo>&#8594;</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>M</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <msub> <mrow> <mtext>SO</mtext></mrow> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <mo>+</mo><msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext></mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mtext>mol</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mfenced> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> <mtext>mol</mtext></mrow> </mfenced></mrow> </math> So, one mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> is required to react with one mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MOH</mi></math>.

So, <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> mole of <math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>2</mtext> </msub> <msub> <mrow> <mtext>SO</mtext></mrow> <mtext>4</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </math> is required to react with one mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>MOH</mi></math>.

</details>

### Q111. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The acidic, basic and amphoteric oxides, respectively, are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>10</mn></msub></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Non-metal oxides are acidic in nature as they produce acidic solution by reacting with water, so here <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Cl</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>10</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> are acidic oxides which produce <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HOCl</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><msub><mi>PO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>SO</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> respectively, with water.</p><p>Alkali metal and alkaline earth metal oxides are basic in nature, as they produce metal hydroxide solution with water and metal hydroxides are basic in nature.</p><p>Here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>CaO</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>Li</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>MgO</mi></math> are basic hydroxides which form <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>NaOH</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>LiOH</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Mg</mi><msub><mfenced><mi>OH</mi></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msub></math> respectively, with water.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is amphoteric oxide as it can react either with acid or base to form salt and water.</p>

</details>

### Q112. JEE Main 2020 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> has a smaller first ionization enthalpy than <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>. Consider the following statement:
(I) it is easier to remove <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> electron than<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>  electron
(II) <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> electron of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is more shielded from the nucleus by the inner core of electrons than the <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> electrons of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
(III) <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math> electron has more penetration power than <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> electron
(IV) atomic radius of <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> is more than <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
(atomic number <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> )
The correct statements are.

**Options:**

- **(A)** (I), (II) and (IV)
- **(B)** (II), (III) and (IV)
- **(C)** (I), (II) and (III)
- **(D)** (I), (III) and (IV)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) (I), (II) and (III)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Since, Penetration of s orbital is the maximum because of the closeness to the nucleus than the p, d and f orbitals
 it is easier to remove <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math> electron than <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></math>  electron
<math> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>Z</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>eff</mtext></mrow> </msub> <mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mtext>shielding&#160;effect</mtext></mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <msub> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>Z</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>eff</mtext></mrow> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mtext>B</mtext> </msub> <mo>&#62;</mo><msub> <mrow> <mfenced> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>Z</mtext> <mrow> <mtext>eff</mtext></mrow> </msub> </mrow> </mfenced></mrow> <mrow> <mtext>Be</mtext></mrow> </msub> </mrow> </math> So, shielding effect is more on 2p electron of B than 2s electron of Be

So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mfenced><mtext>Be</mtext></mfenced><mrow><mtext>I</mtext><msub><mtext>.E</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mfenced><mtext>B</mtext></mfenced><mrow><mtext>I</mtext><msub><mtext>.E</mtext><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></msub></math>

</details>

---

### Q113. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The increasing order of the atomic radii of the following elements is:
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi></mi><mi>C</mi></math>
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi></mi><mi>O</mi></math>
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi></mi><mi>F</mi></math>
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi></mi><mi>C</mi><mi>l</mi></math>
<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi></mi><mi>B</mi><mi>r</mi></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&lt;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

### Q114. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The third ionization enthalpy is minimum for:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math> <mrow> <mi>C</mi><mi>O</mi><mo>−</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow> </mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup> <mi>d</mi> <mn>7</mn> </msup> <mn>4</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>−</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow> </mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup> <mi>d</mi> <mn>6</mn> </msup> <mn>4</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>
 
<math> <mrow> <mi>N</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow> </mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup> <mi>d</mi> <mn>8</mn> </msup> <mn>4</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mi>M</mi><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["> <mrow> <mi>A</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow> </mfenced><mn>3</mn><msup> <mi>d</mi> <mn>5</mn> </msup> <mn>4</mn><msup> <mi>s</mi> <mn>1</mn> </msup> </mrow> </math>

In Fe third electron will remove from 3d and extra stability will be attained by this so ionisation energy is minimum for Fe.

</details>

### Q115. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The first ionization energy (in <math><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></mrow></math> ) of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> <em>and</em> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math> respectively are:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>496</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>737</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>577</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>786</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>496</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>577</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>737</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>786</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>786</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>737</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>577</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>496</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>496</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>577</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>786</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>737</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>496</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>737</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>577</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi><mn>786</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Correct order of ionisation energy will be : Na &lt; Al &lt; Mg &lt; Si.</p><p>On going from left to right in periodic table effective nuclear charge increases due to which ionisation energy increases. But there is exception in order of Al and Mg, in case of Mg electron is removed from fulfilled &#39;s&#39; subshell due to which it has higher I.E.</p>

</details>

### Q116. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Within each pair of elements <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> , <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math> , and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>Li</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Na</mi></math> , respectively, the elements that release more energy upon an electron gain are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math> and <math><mi mathvariant="normal">L</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

(i) Electron affinity of second period <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math>-block element is less than third period <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math>-block element due to small size of second period <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></math>-block element, due to small size electron density is more and upcoming electron face extra repulsion, so energy release is less. Hence, EA order: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Cl</mi></math>.

(ii) Down the group electron affinity decreases due to size increases. EA order: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Li</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Na</mi></math>.

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The electron gain enthalpy (in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>kJ</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> ) of fluorine, chlorine, bromine and iodine, respectively, are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>296</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>325</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>333</mn></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>349</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>349</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>333</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>325</mn></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>296</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>333</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>349</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>325</mn></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>296</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>333</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>325</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>349</mn></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>296</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mo>-</mo><mn>333</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>349</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mo>-</mo><mn>325</mn></math> and <math><mo>-</mo><mn>296</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Order of electron gain enthalpy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>Br</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math>

Electron gain enthalpy of third period elements are greater than second period elements.</p><p>Electron gain enthalpy decrease down to the group, but here Electron gain enthalpy of chlorine is greater than fluorine, it is due to small size of fluorine atom, due to which electron density is high than chlorine, hence upcoming electron face high repulsion in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi></math> atom, hence the energy released is less than chlorine.</p>

</details>

### Q118. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

In comparison to boron, berylium has:

**Options:**

- **(A)** greater nuclear charge and lesser first ionisation enthalpy
- **(B)** lesser nuclear charge and greater first ionisation enthalpy
- **(C)** lesser nuclear charge and lesser first ionisation enthalpy.
- **(D)** greater nuclear charge and greater first ionisation enthalpy.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) lesser nuclear charge and greater first ionisation enthalpy**

**Detailed Solution:**

Electronic configuration of Be <math><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Electronic configuration of B <math><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Due to full-filled configuration of Beryllium, it has higher first ionization energy.
Effective nuclear charge
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub></math> of Be <math><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.85</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0.35</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2.05</mn></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub></math> of B <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#963;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0.85</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0.35</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2.6</mn></math>
<math><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub></math> of B &gt; <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></mrow></msub></math> of Be

</details>

### Q119. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order of the first ionization enthalpies is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we observe from left to right in a period, there is a gradual increase in ionization potential due to the increase in Nuclear charge.
From left to right, the elements will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>Ti</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mn>22</mn></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>Mn</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mn>25</mn></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>Zn</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>)</mo></math> .
Hence, the correct order will be:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ti</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">M</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ni</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q120. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The element having the greatest difference between its first and second ionization energies, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to given options, except for potassium and Sc rest, others are alkaline earth metals with an outer configuration <math><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>.
In the case of potassium, the outer configuration is <math><mn>1</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.
First ionization enthalpy is low, but once an electron is removed, it will attain noble gas configuration and will become more stable. Hence, removing the second electron is difficult and requires a large amount of energy.

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of atomic radii is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math > <mrow>  <mtext>Ce&#x003E;Eu&#x003E;Ho&#x003E;N</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(B)** <math > <mrow>  <mtext>N&#x003E;Ce&#x003E;Eu&#x003E;Ho</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math > <mrow>  <mtext>Ho&#x003E;N&#x003E;Eu&#x003E;Ce</mtext></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math > <mrow>  <mtext>Eu&#x003E;Ce&#x003E;Ho&#x003E;N</mtext></mrow></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math > <mrow>  <mtext>Eu&#x003E;Ce&#x003E;Ho&#x003E;N</mtext></mrow></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As the number of electrons are increased it increases the radius of the element.  As the number of protons are increased the radius is decreased.</p><p>As we can see:</p><p>Radius of :</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Eu</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>199</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pm</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>Ce</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>183</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pm</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>Ho</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>176</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pm</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>170</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>pm</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q123. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct option with respect to the Pauling electronegativity values of the elements is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Te}>\mathrm{Se}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Ga}  < \mathrm{Ge}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Si}  < \mathrm{Al}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{P}>\mathrm{S}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{Ga}  < \mathrm{Ge}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Correct order of electronegativity values of the elements is

$\mathrm{Si}>\mathrm{Al} ; \mathrm{S}>\mathrm{P} ; \mathrm{Se}>\mathrm{Te} ; \mathrm{Ge}>\mathrm{Ga}$

</details>

### Q124. JEE Main 2019 (11 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The correct order of the atomic radii of $\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{Cs}, \mathrm{Al},$ and $\mathrm{S}$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{C}  < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{A} 1 < \mathrm{Cs}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{S}  < \mathrm{C} < \mathrm{Cs} < \mathrm{Al}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{S}  < \mathrm{C} < \mathrm{Al} < \mathrm{Cs}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{C}  < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{Cs} < \mathrm{Al}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{C}  < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{A} 1 < \mathrm{Cs}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

On going down the group, size increases while going from left to right in a period size decreases, so order is

$\mathrm{C}  < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{Al} < \mathrm{Cs}$

</details>

### Q125. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When the first electron gain enthalpy <math> <mrow> <mrow><mo>(</mo> <mrow> <mi>&#916;</mi><msub> <mi>H</mi> <mrow> <mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow> </msub> </mrow> <mo>)</mo></mrow></mrow> </math> of oxygen is <math><mo>-</mo><mn>141</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mrow><mrow><mi>k</mi><mi>J</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></mrow></math> , its second electron gain enthalpy is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** A positive value
- **(B)** Almost the same as that of the first
- **(C)** Negative, but less negative than the first
- **(D)** A more negative value than the first

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) A positive value**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Second electron gain enthalpy is always positive for every element because to overcome repulsion between an anion and electron,  energy has to be supplied.

<math> <mrow> <msup> <mi>O</mi> <mo>&#8722;</mo> </msup> <mo>+</mo><msup> <mi>e</mi> <mo>&#8722;</mo> </msup> <mo>&#8594;</mo><msup> <mi>O</mi> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </msup> <mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mo>;</mo><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi>&#916;</mi><msub> <mi>H</mi> <mrow> <mi>e</mi><mi>g</mi></mrow> </msub> <mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi></mrow> </math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q126. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Aluminium is usually found in <math><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> oxidation state. In contrast, thallium exists in <math><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></math> oxidation states. This is due to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Diagonal relationship
- **(B)** Lattice effect
- **(C)** Lanthanoid contraction
- **(D)** Inert pair effect

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Inert pair effect**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reluctance in tendency of s-electrons to participate in bonding on moving top to bottom in p-block elements is called inert pair effect. Due to inert pair effect <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></math> is very unstable in aluminium.

</details>

### Q127. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In general, the properties that decrease and increase down a group in the periodic table, respectively, are :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Atomic radius and electronegativity
- **(B)** Electronegativity and atomic radius
- **(C)** Electronegativity and electron gain enthalpy
- **(D)** Electron gain enthalpy and electronegativity

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) Electronegativity and atomic radius**

**Detailed Solution:**

(i) Electronegativity: Top to bottom decrease.

(ii) Atomic Radius: generally Increases top to Bottom.

(iii) Electron affinity: generally top to bottom decreases.

</details>

### Q128. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 2 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The correct order of electron affinity is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{Cl}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{Cl}$
- **(C)** F $>\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{O}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{O}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{O}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

On moving from left to right across a period, the electron affinity becomes more negative. On moving from top to bottom in a group, the electron affinity becomes less negative.
Chlorine has more negative electron affinity than fluorine, Because adding an electron to fluorine ( $2 p$ orbital) causes greater repulsion than adding an electron to chlorine $(3 p$ orbital) which is larger in size.

</details>

### Q129. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 1 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

For $\mathrm{Na}^{+}, \mathrm{Mg}^{2+}, \mathrm{F}^{-}$and $\mathrm{O}^{2-}$; the correct order of increasing Ionic radii is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{F}^{-} < \mathrm{Na}^{+} < \mathrm{Mg}^{2+}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Na}^{+} < \mathrm{Mg}^{2+} < \mathrm{F}^{-} < \mathrm{O}^{2-}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} < \mathrm{Na}^{+} < \mathrm{F}^{-} < \mathrm{O}^{2-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} < \mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{Na}^{+} < \mathrm{F}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} < \mathrm{Na}^{+} < \mathrm{F}^{-} < \mathrm{O}^{2-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

All species are isoelectronic $\left(10 \mathrm{e}^{-}\right)$. 
isoelectronic series, when negative charge increases the radius of ion increases.
$\therefore \mathrm{Mg}^{2+} < \mathrm{Na}^{+} < \mathrm{F}^{-} < \mathrm{O}^{2-}$

</details>
