### Q131. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which of the following atoms has the highest first ionization energy?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sc</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Rb</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Na</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sc</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Ionisation energy decreases with increase in the size of atom. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Na</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Rb</mi></math> belongs to alkali metal and down the group ionization energy decreases as size of atom increases down the group.  Among the given elements, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Sc</mi></math> being small has the highest ionization energy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>Na</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>496</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>419</mn><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Sc</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>631</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q132. JEE Main 2014 (19 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which one of the following has largest ionic radius ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>Li</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

With increase in +ve charge, size of cation decreases.
With increase in -ve charge, size of anion increases.
Across the period, ionic size, decreases. 
So correct order is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>Li</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">F</mi><mo>-</mo></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q133. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which of the following arrangements represents the increasing order (smallest to largest) of ionic radii of the given species $\mathrm{O}^{2-}, \mathrm{S}^{2-}, \mathrm{N}^{3-}, \mathrm{P}^{3-}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{N}^{3-} < \mathrm{S}^{2-} < \mathrm{P}^{3-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{P}^{3-} < \mathrm{N}^{3-} < \mathrm{S}^{2-}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{N}^3 < \mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{P}^{3-} < \mathrm{S}^{2-}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{N}^{3-} < \mathrm{S}^{2-} < \mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{P}^{3-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{N}^{3-} < \mathrm{S}^{2-} < \mathrm{P}^{3-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For isoelectronic species ionic radii decreases as the charge on ion decreases. Further on moving down in a group ionic radii increases. Hence the correct order is $\mathrm{O}^{2-} < \mathrm{N}^{3-} < \mathrm{S}^{2-} < \mathrm{P}^{3-}$

</details>

                                  Aluminate anion

[Al(H<sub>2</sub>O)<sub>6</sub>]<sup>3+</sup> is octahedral complex ion

### Q136. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which one of the following cannot function as an oxidising agent?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{I}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{s})$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{NO}_3^{-}(\mathrm{aq})$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cr}_2 \mathrm{O}_7^{2-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{I}^{-}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

e.g. I ${ }^{-}$

### Q137. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The catenation tendency of $\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{Si}$ and $\mathrm{Ge}$ is in the order $\mathrm{Ge} < \mathrm{Si} < \mathrm{C}$. The bond energies (in $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ) of $\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{Si}-\mathrm{Si}$ and $\mathrm{Ge}-\mathrm{Ge}$ bonds are respectively ;

**Options:**

- **(A)** $348,297,260$
- **(B)** $297,348,260$
- **(C)** $348,260,297$
- **(D)** $260,297,348$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $348,297,260$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The linking of identical atoms with each other to form long chains is called catenation. However, this property decreases from carbon to lead. Decrease of this property is associated with $\mathrm{M}-\mathrm{M}$ bond energy which decreases from carbon to lead.

</details>

### Q138. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which is the correct order of second ionization potential of $\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{N}, \mathrm{O}$ and $\mathrm{F}$ in the following ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** F $>$ O $>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $\quad$ C $>$ N $>\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{F}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The second ionization potential means removal of electron from cation $\mathrm{C}^{+}=1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^1, \mathrm{~N}^{+}=1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^2$ $\mathrm{O}^{+}=1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^3, \mathrm{~F}^{+}=1 s^2 2 s^2 2 p^4$ Therefore $\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{C}$

</details>

---

### Q139. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The order of increasing sizes of atomic radii among the elements O, $\mathrm{S}$, Se and As is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** As $ < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{Se}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Se} < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{As} < \mathrm{O}$
- **(C)** O $ < $ S $ < $ As $ < \mathrm{Se}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{O} < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{Se} < \mathrm{As}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) O $ < $ S $ < $ As $ < \mathrm{Se}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

On moving down in a group atomic radii increases due to successive addition of extra shell hence
$$
\mathrm{O} < \mathrm{S} < \text { Se }
$$
Further As is in group 15 having one less electron in its $p$ orbital hence have higher atomic radii than group 16 elements.
i.e., $\mathrm{O} < \mathrm{S} < \mathrm{Se} < \mathrm{As}$

</details>

### Q140. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Electron gain enthalpy with negative sign of fluorine is less than that of chlorine due to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** High ionization enthalpy of fluorine
- **(B)** Smaller size of chlorine atom
- **(C)** Smaller size of fluorine atom
- **(D)** Bigger size of $2 p$ orbital of fluorine

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Smaller size of fluorine atom**

**Detailed Solution:**

The electron gain enthalpy order for halogens is
$\mathrm{Cl}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{Br}>\mathrm{I}$
Due to small size of fluorine the extra electron to be added feels more electronelectron repulsion. Therefore fluorine has less value for electron affinity than chlorine.

</details>

### Q141. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which of the following represents the correct order of increasing first ionization enthalpy for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ba</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ar</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ba</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ar</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ba</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ar</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ba</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ar</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ba</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ar</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ba</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>Ar</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Ba</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ar</mi></math></p><p>
Ba and Ca belong to group IIA ( alkaline earth metals).

Se and S belong to group Vl A (Chalcogens) and as we go down the group ionization enthalpy decreases with increasing atomic size. So,
         
          <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ba</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Ca</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and&#160;</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Se</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#60;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math>
Ar has stable completely filled electronic configuration and, therefore, has the highest ionization enthalpy.Hence, the correct order is

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable equalcolumns="true" equalrows="true"><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mi>Ba</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#60;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>Ca</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#60;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>Se</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#60;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>&#60;</mo></mtd><mtd><mi>Ar</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mtd><mtd><mn>503</mn></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd><mn>590</mn></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd><mn>941</mn></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd><mn>999</mn></mtd><mtd></mtd><mtd><mn>1520</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q142. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The first ionisation potential of Na is 5.1 eV. The value of electron gain enthalpy of Na <sup>+</sup> will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** -10.2 eV
- **(B)** +2.55 eV
- **(C)** -2.55 eV
- **(D)** -5.1 eV

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) -5.1 eV**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Electron affinity is defined as the change in energy (in kJ/mole) of a neutral atom (in the gaseous phase) when an electron is added to the atom to form a negative ion.</p><p>In other words, the neutral atom&#39;s likelihood of gaining an electron.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Na</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#8594;</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><msub><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>;</mo></math>
<math> <mrow> <msup> <mn>1</mn> <mrow> <mi>s</mi><mi>t</mi> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math> ionisation energy ; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mtext>&#8201;</mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.1</mo><mi>eV</mi></math>
Electron gaining is the reversed reaction of ionization. Hence, electron gain enthalpy is ; </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>IE</mi><mi>Na</mi></msub></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>EA</mi><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup></msub></mfenced></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><msup><mi>Na</mi><mo>+</mo></msup><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mo>&#8594;</mo><mtext>&#8201;&#8201;</mtext><mi>Na</mi></math>
So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>eg</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>.1</mo><mi>eV</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q143. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Which of the following presents the correct order of second ionization enthalpies of $\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{N}, \mathrm{O}$ and $\mathrm{F}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** F $>\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** O $>$ N $>$ F $>$ C
- **(C)** $\mathrm{C}>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{F}$
- **(D)** O $>$ F $>$ N $>$ C

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) O $>$ F $>$ N $>$ C**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a period the value of ionisation potential increases from left to right with breaks where the atoms have some what stable configuration. After removing an $e^{-}$ oxygen will acquire stable configuration. Hence $\mathrm{O}^{+}$has highest second ionisation enthalpy than $\mathrm{F}^{+}$and $\mathrm{N}^{+}$.
$\therefore$ correct order is $\mathrm{O}>\mathrm{F}>\mathrm{N}>\mathrm{C}$

</details>

### Q144. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which one of the following will react most vigorously with water?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Li}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{K}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Rb}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Na}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{Rb}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reactivity with water increases on moving down the Goup from $\mathrm{Li}$ to $\mathrm{Cs}$ as $\mathrm{Li} < \mathrm{Na}$ $ < \mathrm{K} < \mathrm{Rb} < \mathrm{Cs}$. This is due to increase in electropositive character. Hence Rb reacts most vigorously with water.

</details>

---

### Q145. JEE Main 2012 (12 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which among the following elements has the highest first ionization enthalpy?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Nitrogen
- **(B)** Boron
- **(C)** Carbon
- **(D)** Oxygen

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Nitrogen**

**Detailed Solution:**

Due to stable $2 s^2 2 p^3$ configuration. (half filled $p$-orbital) Nitrogen atom has highest energy.

</details>

### Q146. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

In which of the following arrangements, the sequence is not strictly according to the property written against it?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{CO}_2 < \mathrm{SiO}_2 < \mathrm{SnO}_2 < \mathrm{PbO}_2$ : increasing oxidising power
- **(B)** $\mathrm{NH}_3 < \mathrm{PH}_3 < \mathrm{AsH}_3 < \mathrm{SbH}_3$ : increasing basic strength
- **(C)** $\mathrm{HF} < \mathrm{HCl} < \mathrm{HBr} < \mathrm{HI}$ : increasing acid strength
- **(D)** $\mathrm{B} < \mathrm{C} < \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{N}$ : increasing first ionisation enthalpy.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{NH}_3 < \mathrm{PH}_3 < \mathrm{AsH}_3 < \mathrm{SbH}_3$ : increasing basic strength**

**Detailed Solution:**

Correct order of increasing basic strength is $\mathrm{NH}_3>\mathrm{PH}_3>\mathrm{AsH}_3>\mathrm{SbH}_3>\mathrm{BiH}_3$

</details>

### Q147. JEE Main 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Which one of the following order represents the correct sequence of the increasing basic nature of the given oxides ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{MgO} < \mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3 < \mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{O}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{MgO} < \mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3 < \mathrm{MgO}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3 < \mathrm{MgO} < \mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{O}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3 < \mathrm{MgO} < \mathrm{Na}_2 \mathrm{O} < \mathrm{K}_2 \mathrm{O}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Across a period metallic strength decreases $\&$ down the group it increases

</details>

### Q151. JEE Main 2005

In which of the following arrangements the order is NOT according to the property indicated against it?

- **(A)** $$
\mathrm{Al}^{3+} < \mathrm{Mg}^{2+} < \mathrm{Na}^{+} < \mathrm{F}^{-}
$$
Increasing ionic size
- **(B)** $$
\mathrm{B} < \mathrm{C} < \mathrm{N} < \mathrm{O}
$$
Increasing first ionization enthalpy
- **(C)** $$
\mathrm{l} < \mathrm{Br} < \mathrm{F} < \mathrm{Cl}

Increasing electron gain enthalpy (with negative sign)

$$
Increasing first ionization enthalpy**

**Detailed Solution:**

B  < C < O < N

</details>

### Q154. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The radionucleide ${ }_{90}^{234} \mathrm{Th}$ undergoes two successive $\beta$-decays followed by one $\alpha$-decay. The atomic number and the mass number respectively of the resulting radionucleide are

**Options:**

- **(A)** 94 and 230
- **(B)** 90 and 230
- **(C)** 92 and 230
- **(D)** 92 and 234

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 90 and 230**

**Detailed Solution:**

${ }_{90}^{234} \mathrm{Th} \stackrel{-\beta}{\longrightarrow}{ }_{91}^{234} \mathrm{X} \stackrel{-\beta}{\longrightarrow}{ }_{92}^{234} \mathrm{Th}--\alpha{ }_{90}^{230} \mathrm{Th}$

</details>

- **(D)** $\beta$-particle is not emitted

${ }_0 n^1 \rightarrow_{+1} p^1+_{-1} e^0$
